科技動能支撐股市，利率風險牽動債市配置
2026 年 9 月
全球股市 8 月截至 26 日多數上漲，其中成熟市場上漲 3.1%居冠，新興亞洲、東協股市與標普
500 也分別上漲 2.9%、2.7%及 2.6%。美股受企業財報韌性與月初就業、通膨數據降溫支撐；亞洲
則受美元偏弱、油價與長端殖利率回落帶動風險偏好改善。然而，中國股市期間下跌 0.3%，該國 7
月零售、工業生產與投資數據偏弱，加上阿里巴巴折價配股一度衝擊科技股，惟公司高層後續增持有
助緩解信心壓力 。 拉美股市下跌0.8%，在主要股市中敬陪末座 ， 主要拖累來自巴西 ；8 月截至 24 日，

外資淨流出巴西股市約 214 億雷亞爾，加上財政與 10 月大選的不確定性壓抑市場。印度亦小跌
0.3%，因高油價與 RBI 偏鷹訊號使公債殖利率上升。
全球債市 8 月截至 26 日則全面收紅 ， 以新興當地債上漲1.53%居冠 ， 美元指數回落至99 附近，
為其帶來匯兌加成；亞洲高收益債與全球高收益債分別上漲 1.16%、1.09%，高票息與較短存續期提
供支撐 。 亞洲債券 、 全球主權債與新興美元債表現相對落後 。8 月中美國 30 年期殖利率一度升至 2007
年以來高點，壓抑長存續期債券；其後油價回落，加上8/19 財政部宣布 9/9 起將 10 至 30 年期公債
買回上限至少倍增至每次 40 億美元，長端流動性疑慮獲緩解，30 年期殖利率回落，因而帶動債券價
格修復。
展望 9 月，市場焦點將集中於 9 月 15 日至 16 日的 FOMC 會議，以及最新經濟預測與點陣圖；
全球股市預料呈現高檔震盪格局。儘管物價漲勢稍有緩和，但 Fed 對通膨仍保持警戒；若政策立場
較市場預期鷹派，可能推升美債殖利率波動，並壓抑高估值成長股走勢。不過，AI 基礎建設與企業

科技投資趨勢延續，美國企業獲利成長亦有利科技股表現，預料中期基本面仍具支撐。台灣受 AI 與
半導體出口需求帶動，經濟成長動能強勁；日股則持續受企業治理改革與資本效率改善支撐。整體而
言，9 月股市短線震盪難免，但中期仍看好科技股，以及基本面相對強勁的台灣與日本股市。
就債市而言，美債殖利率預料仍將處於相對高檔；若就業持續降溫且通膨未再明顯升高，中期利
率上行空間可望受限。期限配置方面，短端對 Fed 政策預期較敏感，過短的存續期間亦伴隨再投資
風險；10 年以上債券則持續承受財政與期限溢酬壓力。相較之下，3 至 10 年期債券兼具較高息收、
適度存續期間與參與利率下行的空間。整體而言，目前 3 至 10 年期美國公債及投資級債的風險報酬
條件相對均衡，較具表現機會。

美國股市：AI 熱潮推升企業獲利，美股續強但留意利率風險
八月美股在高檔震盪中收高，科技股表現亮眼，除整體美股優異財報支撐指數表現外，AI 龍頭企
業如 NVIDIA 均給出強勁展望 ，AI 行情由晶片硬體向軟體與資安應用端擴散 。指數上揚主要由輝達、
微軟、亞馬遜、帕蘭提爾、特斯拉、Meta、美光科技、賽富時、Marvell、默克藥廠等企業帶動，表
現相對弱勢的族群包括蘋果、博通、沃爾瑪、TJX、AppLovin、威騰、聯合健康、CVS、應用材料、
超微半導體等。
聯準會新任主席試圖以新的溝通模式取代傳統前瞻指引，市場解讀為基準利率有上行空間且更長

時間維持高檔。聯準會主席華許在全球央行年會(Jackson Hole)拒絕提供傳統的前瞻指引或機械式的
反應函數，強調央行過度承諾未來利率路徑會失去政策彈性，以一套「決策準則」取代指引，重點觀
察潛在通膨是否快速回到 2%、勞動市場是否偏離充分就業，以及金融條件是否有效限制總需求。由
於 7 月 FOMC 會議出現官員主張升息的情況，使短端與長端美債殖利率走勢分化，殖利率曲線進一
步陡峭化 ， 市場正在質疑 Fed 是否具備一致且可理解的決策框架 ， 但在華許發言後 ， 按照這套標準，
目前政策訊號仍偏向利率在較高水準維持更長時間。由於通膨問題仍具廣度，同時信用利差偏低、股
市高檔、AI 投資與私人需求持續強勁，很難將目前金融條件視為具有限制性，故市場預期 聯準會可
能不只提高升息機率，也提高了升息後維持高利率的可能性，甚至進一步提高未來降息的門檻。
第二季財報公布大致落幕，表現優於先前預期。目前標普 500 成分股已有 459 家公布 2Q26 財
報，其中 86%獲利優於市場預期，並帶動 2Q26 獲利年增達約 31.6%，較季初市場預估的 21%大幅

上修；全年獲利年增亦由 23%上修至 27%。類股獲利年增動能以能源 (YoY+145%)、資訊科技
(YoY+71%)、原材料(YoY+41%)增長動能最強，醫療保健類股(YoY-7%)表現落後。資訊科技類股獲
利成長主要由半導體族群帶動，次產業中半導體獲利年增 139%、科技硬體與設備獲利年增 50.9%。
美國期中選舉前的「資料中心用電排擠效應引發的民粹反彈」持續發酵。隨著 AI 資料中心龐大
的電力需求，引發地方民意對民生與傳統產業用電資源遭擠壓的疑慮持續升溫 。 各州政府在選舉壓力
下，部分地方政府緊急凍結資料中心計畫，預料 11 月期中選舉後政治干擾將可降溫。
目前標普 500 指數位於歷史高檔位置，本益比已升至 23.51 倍、股價淨值比達 5.6 倍，短期評價
偏高。雖然美國科技業者目前仍居 AI 產業領先地位，但股市漲幅已高，短期仍需留意市場過熱與估
值偏高風險，建議以長線角度分批布局。

歐洲股市：景氣溫和復甦，歐洲央行升息預期與政治變數限制評價 
八月歐股延續多頭走勢，泛歐600 指數中原物料與科技股表現相對強勢，包括思愛普、艾司摩爾
控股、Argenx、施耐德電機、力拓、嘉能可、英美資源、安聯集團、勞斯萊斯控股、西門子等表現
較佳；相較之下，部分金融與消費股表現偏弱，包括雀巢、匯豐控股、諾和諾德、英美菸草、阿斯特
捷利康、法國巴黎銀行、法國興業銀行、諾華、百威英博、萬喜等。 
歐元區八月綜合 PMI 升至 52.1，連續第 2 個月站上榮枯線，顯示第 3 季初景氣延續擴張。其中
製造業升至 52.8，創 2022 年 5 月以來最佳表現，復甦已由生產端擴散至需求端。AI 相關科技與設
備需求增加，加上國防支出擴大，帶動製造業需求回升；服務業則持平於 51.7，受惠於旅遊維持溫
和擴張。國家別來看，德國製造業 PMI 升至 54.1，創 2022 年以來最佳表現，法國製造業重返擴張
區間，但極端高溫拖累服務業活動。整體而言，八月 PMI 顯示歐元區景氣動能明顯改善，製造業加
速復甦，短期衰退風險進一步下降，然而服務業擴張力道未見加快，復甦步調呈現分化。 
部分歐洲央行官員表示，九月已有充分理由升息，利率交換市場目前反映 ECB 在 9 月會議升息
一碼的可能性。美伊戰爭推高能源成本，加上歐洲經濟表現穩健，使市場認為 ECB 仍有收緊貨幣政
策的空間。市場焦點因此由「ECB 會不會升息」轉向「升息循環還會延續多久」 。中東局勢仍是最大
變數，若能源成本持續走高且逐步傳導至商品、服務價格與薪資，ECB 第四季仍可能維持鷹派立場。 
9 月歐股須留意政治局勢。若 AfD（德國另類選擇黨）於地方選舉取得大勝，雖不會直接改變目
前政府執政地位，但可能削弱改革推進速度，造成市場風險溢價上升；另外英國新任首相柏南承諾維
持既有財政規則，並受到工黨不提高所得稅、國民保險及主要稅率承諾的約束，使政府在國防、公共
服務與生活成本支出增加之際 ， 財政操作空間相當有限 ， 市場因此高度關注政府是否轉向其他形式的
增稅。若改革措施過度激進推高英國公債殖利率，恐將造成市場震盪。 
歐洲經濟整體呈現溫和復甦，但原油價格再次走高是最大隱憂。若能源成本居高不下，歐洲央行
與英國央行第四季仍可能維持鷹派立場 ， 進而導致產業表現分化 。 銀行與金融類股可望受惠於利率走
高及淨利差改善；反之，科技、醫療保健及其他成長型類股將面臨殖利率上升所帶來的評價壓力。目
前泛歐 600 指數本益比約 16.5 倍，股價淨值比約 2.5 倍。雖然俄烏戰爭與通膨再度升溫風險仍需關
注，但在評價合理的背景下，歐股仍適合作為長線分批布局標的。

資料來源：彭博,2026/8/31

日本股市：通膨回溫拉高升息預期，半導體與內需動能推升經濟表現
八月日股受地緣政治風險與殖利率走高影響，日經225 指數劇烈震盪。盤面上，強勢族群包括瑞
可利控股、愛德萬測試、藤倉、軟銀、松下控股、村田製作所、瑞穗金融、東京威力科創、三菱UFJ
金融、揖斐電等；弱勢股包括東京海上控股、迅銷、大金工業、富士軟片、日本電裝、發那科、7&I、
丸紅、損保控股、KDDI 等。
8 月東京核心 CPI 升至 1.8%，雖能源補貼與食品價格降溫壓抑整體物價，但租金與醫療服務價
格明顯上升；扣除食品與能源後通膨加速至 1.4%，顯示內生性通膨壓力仍在。若就十大產品分項來

看，引導本月通膨的上漲主要來自房屋及醫療保健，其中租金價格年增幅達 1.9%，反映物價上漲持
續傳遞至房市 ； 醫療保健價格年增幅亦自前月的1.4%攀升至 2.8%， 因政府將高額療養費制度內的自
付費用上限調高所致 。 根據總務省估算 ， 日本政府對能源補貼的政策約壓抑本月通膨0.27 個百分點，
顯示若扣除政策影響，則當前通膨約為 2.2%，仍高於日本央行所設定的物價目標。雖政府補貼政策
將持續對通膨帶來壓抑效果，使得整體通膨略低於 2%，然而搭配央行對日圓匯率過於弱勢、恐進一
步墊高通膨的擔憂，預估將促使日本央行最快於 9 月份升息一碼。
日本半導體製造裝置協會 （SEAJ） 公布7 月日本製晶片設備銷售額 （3 個月移動平均值） 為5,562.2
億日圓，月銷售額年增 35.4%，創下 2024 年 12 月以來最大增幅。同時，若觀察今年 1 至 7 月份累
計銷售額達 3.44 兆日圓，年增 15.7%，顯示隨著當前人工智慧墊高晶片需求，推升廠商對半導體設
備的需求。
日本 7 月外國旅客入境人數為 344.2 萬人次，年增 0.1%。本月份外國旅客人數最大宗仍是來自

南韓，達 89.5 萬人，年增 31.9%。其次則為台灣遊客的 76.4 萬人次，年增 26.4%。與之相反，來
自中國的遊客人數年減幅達 56.1%， 因日中兩國關係遲遲未見改善所致 。 根據日本百貨店協會公布的
資料顯示，7 月百貨公司免稅銷售額年增 25.5%至 506 億日圓，連續五個月的正成長，顯示在日圓
延續弱勢的格局之下，外國旅客消費力仍有提升空間。
2026 年來日股 TOPIX 指數受惠於 AI 題材、日圓貶值、高市擴大財政刺激與強化軍事安全等政
策利多支撐，雖然日圓在政府干預與 BOJ 升息下繼續貶值空間受限，出口企業的匯兌紅利將逐步消
退，但預料日圓接下來仍以區間整理機會較高，並無大幅升值空間。目前 TOPIX 指數本益比約 17.7
倍、股價淨值比約 1.9 倍，評價仍屬合理，可利用市場震盪機會逢低分批布局。

台灣股市：工業生產創高、景氣續亮紅燈，AI 供應鏈撐起製造業擴張動能
8 月台股在 AI 龍頭財報優於預期及全球 AI 資本支出持續擴張帶動下，市場氣氛維持偏多，但受
到 Jackson Hole 會議後市場重新調整聯準會政策預期、美債殖利率攀升及美元轉強影響，指數於高
檔呈現震盪整理。資金面上，高評價 AI 族群漲多後出現輪動，市場開始重新檢視企業未來獲利成長
能否持續支撐評價水準。不過整體而言，AI 資料中心、半導體、先進封裝及伺服器供應鏈需求仍維
持強勁，有助於消化部分評價壓力，並使市場焦點重新回到企業基本面與獲利成長。

當前臺灣經濟仍處於景氣擴張階段。全球 AI 建置需求推動企業設備投資與科技資本支出維持高
檔，相關需求正逐步由晶片擴散至資料中心、網通設備、電力基礎建設及相關零組件。從國際環境觀
察，美國企業投資及私人需求維持韌性，AI 相關支出已成為支撐美國與全球科技景氣的重要力量，
也持續為台灣出口與半導體供應鏈提供結構性成長動能。
展望 9 月，市場關注焦點將由企業財報逐漸轉向總體經濟數據與聯準會政策方向。Jackson Hole
會議後，市場對於聯準會維持較高利率期間的預期升溫，短期可能提高全球金融市場波動。然而，從
企業基本面觀察，NVIDIA 最新展望顯示 AI 需求仍具高度能見度，全球雲端業者與大型企業的 AI 基
礎建設支出並未出現明顯放緩跡象。在此背景下，只要半導體、伺服器及先進封裝產業維持強勁接單
動能，台灣電子產業景氣仍有機會延續高檔表現。
投資策略方面，仍可維持對 AI 供應鏈的正向看法，但須更重視獲利成長與評價合理性。除了先
進製程及先進封裝外，AI 伺服器組裝、散熱、電源管理、高速傳輸、網通設備及記憶體等受惠 AI 基

建擴張之族群，仍具備中長期成長機會。隨著AI 投資逐步由 GPU 擴散至記憶體、網路交換器、光通
訊及系統整合等領域 ， 受惠範圍亦由少數龍頭供應商進一步擴展至整體供應鏈 ， 有助於支撐相關企業
未來營收與獲利成長動能。另一方面，9 月需留意美國 CPI 數據、聯準會利率決策及美債殖利率變化
對高評價科技股的影響；若市場因政策預期、資金輪動或短期獲利了結而出現回檔，較可能屬於多頭
趨勢中的震盪整理，而非基本面反轉。在企業獲利持續成長、AI 資本支出維持高檔及全球科技景氣
延續復甦的背景下 ， 建議布局方向可由單純題材面轉向具備技術門檻 、 訂單能見度及現金流品質較佳
的個股，以提升投資組合在景氣循環與市場波動中的穩健度。

中國股市：硬科技 IPO 熱潮延燒，A 股中報揭露「增收不增利」普遍現象
8 月中國經濟延續「總量承壓、科技產業相對強勢」的分化格局。儘管房地產調整與內需復甦力
道仍有限，但高端製造、人工智慧及數位經濟仍是政策資源與市場資金聚焦方向。在傳統產業景氣復
甦較為緩慢的情況下 ， 科技產業逐漸成為支撐經濟成長的重要動能 ， 顯示中國經濟正持續朝新舊動能
轉換與產業升級方向發展。
科技產業方面，人形機器人已成為近期最受市場關注的新興產業之一。根據中國國家市場監管總
局統計，2026 年上半年人形機器人領域新設企業數量達 11.6 萬家，較 2025 年同期的 7.2 萬家大幅

成長 61.1%，反映資本投入、技術研發與產業參與程度快速提升。隨著人工智慧、大模型及感測技術
進步，人形機器人正逐步由研發驗證邁向商業 化應用階段，產業發展重點也由概念題材轉向量產能
力、產品可靠度及終端需求驗證。
資本市場表現雖顯示科技板塊仍是 A 股資金主要聚焦方向 ， 但企業基本面改善幅度與市場期待之
間仍存在落差。從滬深 300 成分股來看，科技類企業近年營收維持增長，但獲利表現並未同步大幅
提升，反映部分企業仍面臨研發投入增加、產能擴張及市場競爭加劇等壓力。AI、半導體及高端製造
雖持續獲得政策支持與資本關注 ， 但不少企業的估值已提前反映未來成長預期 ， 實際獲利貢獻仍有待
進一步驗證。市場關注焦點也逐漸由政策題材與自主替代概念，轉向訂單能見度、現金流與獲利能力
等基本面指標；若後續營運表現未能跟上市場預期，不排除科技板塊仍面臨評價修正壓力。
展望 9 月，政策方向預計仍以穩增長與培育新質生產力為主軸，人工智慧、人形機器人、高端製
造及半導體等領域仍將是支持重點 。 在整體經濟復甦力道尚未全面擴散的背景下 ， 市場表現預期仍將

呈現結構性分化，資金持續聚焦具成長潛力與技術門檻的科技產業。投資策略上，可留意人形機器人
產業鏈、工業自動化、AI 應用、半導體設備及高端製造等受惠產業升級趨勢之族群；惟部分熱門題
材評價已反映較高成長預期，後續仍須觀察訂單落地、量產進度及獲利改善情況，避免因短期市場情
緒過熱而提高投資風險。

科技類股：AI 基礎建設持續擴張，算力、光通訊與平台生態同步受惠
全球科技產業的核心成長動能仍來自 AI 基礎建設擴張 ， 但市場焦點已逐步由單純GPU 採購轉向
整體資料中心架構升級 。 隨Google 擴大投入光通訊與高速網路技術 ，AI 資料中心正朝向更高頻寬、
更低延遲及更高能效方向發展 。 市場預估800G 至 1.6T 光通訊滲透率將持續提升 ， 推動光模組 、CPO、
交換器及相關供應鏈需求成長，也反映大型雲端服務供應商仍持續強化 AI 基礎建設布局。在此趨勢
下，AI 投資已由單一晶片擴展至網路、儲存、記憶體及先進封裝等完整生態系，帶動相關供應鏈重

新評價。
企業獲利方面，NVIDIA 最新財報再次驗證 AI 投資循環仍具延續性。公司第二季營收與資料中心
業務均大幅優於預期，資料中心網路業務亦維持高速成長，顯示大型企業、雲端服務業者及 AI 模型
開發商對算力需求仍然強勁。同時，管理層對第三季營收及新一代 Vera Rubin 平台維持正向展望，
反映 AI 基礎建設建置並未出現明顯放緩跡象。不過，市場對AI 產業的評價標準已逐漸由需求成長轉
向獲利品質與投資效益，未來資本支出、折舊攤提與現金流變化仍將是投資人關注重點。
從產業鏈角度來看，AI 需求正持續向外擴散。除了 GPU 之外，高速光通訊、網路交換器、高頻
寬記憶體(HBM)、CPU 及資料中心電力架構均成為下一階段重要受惠領域。另一方面，NVIDIA 近期
宣布結合金融機構與雲端服務資源打造大型 AI 基礎設施平台，顯示產業發展正由硬體銷售進一步延
伸至算力服務與平台營運模式。此舉有利強化 AI 產業生態系並提高資本取得效率，但也反映未來競
爭重點將由單純硬體性能逐步轉向平台整合能力、商業模式及資金運用效率。隨著更多企業投入 AI

建設，市場也開始關注高額資本支出是否能持續帶來相應回報，產業獲利表現仍可能出現明顯分化。
展望後續，AI 仍是科技產業最具確定性的投資主軸，但市場關注焦點正由 「建置多少算力」逐步
轉向「創造多少效益」。NVIDIA 新平台推廣進度、Vera Rubin 產品放量、高速光通訊滲透率提升
及 HBM 供給變化，將是觀察下一階段 AI 投資循環的重要指標。雖然大型雲端業者資本支出尚未出
現明顯下修，支撐整體供應鏈需求維持高檔，但隨著資料中心建置成本、能源支出與折舊費用持續增
加 ， 企業獲利能力與現金流表現的重要性亦將提升 。 未來較具競爭優勢的企業 ， 除了掌握核心技術外 ，
更需具備規模化商業模式及資本效率，才能在 AI 產業由高速成長邁向成熟發展的過程中持續創造價
值。

礦業類股：關稅預期有利銅價支撐，戰略庫存需求是潛在利多
8 月以來礦業類股走勢強勁，倫敦金屬交易所銅價也再創歷史新高，這波上漲的主要動能，仍是
來自於我們先前一再提到的美國潛在銅關稅，改變了全球銅庫存的流向 。 由於市場擔心美國未來可能
對精煉銅加徵關稅，交易商提前將銅運往美國，希望在關稅實施前完成進口，導致其他地區能夠取得
的現貨供應相應減少，在供給減少下持續為銅價帶來支撐。
截至 8 月底，美國現行關稅主要涵蓋銅棒、銅線、銅板及銅管等半成品與衍生產品，精煉銅尚未
正式納入課稅範圍，但市場普遍預估美國政府將有可能在 2027 年開始實施精煉銅關稅，帶動交易商

提前採取備貨行動。路透社報導指出，美國2026 年上半年進口約 88.5 萬噸精煉銅，年增約 3%，紐
約商品交易所銅庫存也攀升至約 67.5 萬噸的歷史高點。若將時間拉長至一年觀察，全球三大交易所
合計增加約 49.58 萬噸庫存，其中約 81%集中於紐約商品交易所，顯示全球可見銅庫存雖然上升，
但新增庫存大多被鎖在美國，非美市場銅庫存相對處於較為緊張的狀態。
除了關稅帶動的庫存 失衡外，銅的實際應用需求也具有基本面支撐。彭博行業研究 預估，2026
年全球銅需求將成長 2.4%至 2.7%，增速雖較 2025 年放緩，但電動車、再生能源、資料中心及電網
升級仍將帶動用銅需求上升。其中雖然中國銅需求成長預估也將放緩，但是中國國家電網已規劃 2026
至 2030 年投資 4 兆元人民幣，較前五年增加 44%，以支應人工智慧用電需求。
另一方面，隨著區域銅庫存出現短缺及 AI 帶動用銅需求上升，若政府與企業在未來計畫提高安
全庫存，將可能成為市場尚未充分反映的中長期上漲基礎。花旗研究估算，目前全球精煉銅總庫存約

為 300 萬噸，僅相當於 1.3 個月的全球消費量。若各國政府及大型供應鏈基於國防、能源安全、人
工智慧基礎建設或地緣政治風險 ， 將安全庫存提高至兩個月 ， 市場約需要額外累積 170 萬噸銅庫存；
若提高至三個月，額外需求更將擴大至約 400 萬噸。由於新礦山從開發到投產需要多年，供給無法
在短期內迅速增加，市場只能透過銅價上漲，促使更多廢銅進入市場，同時迫使部分使用者節約用銅
或尋找替代材料。因此，建立庫存的規模愈大、完成時間愈短，銅價需要調整的漲幅也將愈顯著。
展望後市，儘管美國精煉銅關稅目前尚未正式實施，但市場預期帶來的提前備貨潮仍有利短線銅
價支撐，同時全球用銅需求仍維持成長，新礦山供給又難以快速實現，加上政府及企業可能逐步提高
安全庫存 ， 使銅市場的中長期供需結構仍具有支撐 ， 故對具銅價曝險的礦業類股中長期後市仍可正向
看待 ， 然而仍需留意若未來銅關稅未如市場預期實施 ， 當前囤積於美國市場的銅庫存將可能回歸到全
球市場當中，為銅價帶來短線波動。

能源類股：荷莫茲海峽持續受阻，惟實際供應缺口與庫存降幅低於市場預期
8 月以來，美伊衝突持續反覆升降溫，雙方時而釋出談判訊號，時而再度發生軍事衝突，短期內
仍未出現實質進展，荷莫茲海峽通行亦持續受限。然而，海峽受阻並不代表原有運量全數消失，部分
原油仍可經由海運與陸上替代管線出口 ，同時中國石油需求下降及各國政府釋放戰略庫存亦抵銷部分
中斷影響。
彭博行業研究指出，儘管當前荷莫茲海峽受到干擾，但實際並未呈現完全封鎖的狀態，目前仍有
零星油輪持續通行。彭博指出，由於部分油輪會關閉公開定位訊號，並在通過海峽後於阿曼外海進行

轉運，因此公開船舶資料可能會漏算部分運量。彭博綜合目前庫存變化與市場實際供需後，估計目前
每日仍有約 750 萬桶原油通過荷莫茲海峽，意味僅依據可觀察船舶數量推算中東石油供應中斷量，
可能高估了市場實際缺口。
除了每日仍有約 750 萬桶原油經過海峽運輸外，彭博估計目前另有每日約 500 萬桶石油改走陸
上管線；需求端則因中國每日減少約 400 萬桶進口而下降，其他地區減少用油與各國政府釋放戰略
庫存再合計填補每日約 200 萬桶石油缺口。經過上述調整後，原本每日約 2,000 萬桶經荷莫茲海峽
通行的運量中，實際供應缺口僅剩每日約 150 萬桶，這也解釋了當前油價漲幅為何未完全反映表面
的產量損失。
全球石油庫存變化的數據亦呼應了實際缺口低於市場普遍預期的論點。 花旗報告指出 ，2026 年 2
月至 8 月全球實際觀察到的石油供需缺口平均約為每日 290 萬桶，明顯低於其原先預估的每日 700
萬至 800 萬桶。截至 2026 年 8 月，全球石油庫存仍可覆蓋 99 天需求，亦仍處於 2022 年至 2025

年的 95 至 101 天區間。花旗估計，若目前庫存下降速度延續，全球庫存要到 2029 年第一季才會降
至接近第二次石油危機緊張程度的 70 天水位，這顯示荷莫茲海峽若持續受阻，雖油價上漲壓力仍會
逐步累積，但實際庫存下降速度並未如市場普遍預期的嚴峻。
展望後市， 除荷莫茲海峽中斷造成的 供應缺口與庫存降幅低於市場預期 外，國際能源署 也預估
2027 年全球石油需求將增加每日 240 萬桶 ， 供應則可能年增每日830 萬桶 ， 意味若中東產量與海峽
通行逐步恢復，供應回升速度將明顯快於需求，並帶動庫存回補，不利油價上漲趨勢，然而考量短線
地緣風險仍有利能源類股形成支撐，在美伊達成長期停火協議前，應中性看待能源類股後市表現。

黃金類股：央行買盤支撐中長期價格，惟升息預期帶動短線波動升高
8 月金價走勢先揚後抑。月初公布的美國就業數據偏弱，加上中國及全球黃金 ETF 買盤回升，使
市場一度降低近期升息預期，金價因而突破 7 月的整理區間。到了 8 月中旬，美國財政部宣布擴大
長天期公債回購，帶動長端殖利率與美元回落，進一步放大黃金漲勢；不過，月底公布的通膨數據仍
顯示物價壓力具有黏性 ， 聯準會主席華許在傑克森霍爾會議的態度也偏向鷹派 ， 使市場重新提高升息
預期，金價隨即自高檔回落。然而即使月底出現修正，金價在 8 月仍累積明顯漲幅。
支撐金價中長期走勢的核心力量，仍然來自世界各國央行為分散外匯儲備，持續增持黃金。世界

黃金協會指出，2026 年第 2 季全球央行淨購金量為 289 噸，累計 2026 年上半年約 345 噸。雖然上
半年央行購金量受到土耳其、俄羅斯等國調節部位影響，降至2022 年以來同期低點，但第 2 季購金
量已較第 1 季明顯回升，而且買盤仍分布於波蘭、中國、烏茲別克及哈薩克等多個市場，顯示各國央
行增持黃金的趨勢仍未出現轉變，且可觀察到各國央行對於逢低承接黃金的意願。
在央行需求之外，中國投資資金是本輪金價突破的重要邊際買盤。2026 年上半年中國黃金淨進
口量達 764 噸，較 2025 年同期增加 138%，其規模明顯高於中國人民銀行公布的增持量，顯示民間
與機構投資人也在提高黃金配置 。 當中國資金推動金價突破整理區間後 ， 美國商品期貨交易委員會的
資料也顯示 ， 管理基金黃金淨多單在 8 月持續增加 ， 使原本集中於亞洲的漲勢進一步獲得美國期貨資
金支持。
然而，投資資金參與程度提高，也使金價對利率與美元變化更加敏感。美國財政部 8 月 19 日宣
布，將至少加倍 10 年至 30 年期公債的流動性支持回購規模，市場隨即下調長端殖利率，黃金也在

持有成本下降後快速上漲。不過，月底公布的美國 7 月通膨數據仍高，加上華許在傑克森霍爾會議維
持偏鷹立場，再次使市場重新認為聯準會仍可能透過升息抑制通膨，美元與殖利率回升後，部分短線
資金便出現獲利了結賣壓。
展望後市，各國央行增持黃金儲備仍支撐黃金的中長期多頭結構，而美國財政與債務可持續性疑
慮也將持續提高黃金的分散風險價值。不過，8 月底的回檔顯示，當金價漲勢由 ETF 及期貨資金放大
後，實質利率與美元只要轉為上升，短線市場部位便可能迅速調整，故在美國後續利率政策仍具有不
確定性的狀況下，對於黃金類股後市應中性看待。

固定收益：長端風險仍存，3~10 年期公債、投資級債更具表現機會
8 月以來，美國公債殖利率維持高檔震盪，若以 7 月底與 8/24 比較，各年期債券殖利率近乎平
行小幅下行。8 月初公布的 7 月非農就業意外減少 2.3 萬人，顯示勞動市場動能轉弱；7 月 CPI 年增
率由 3.5%降至 3.4%，核心 CPI 年增率亦由 2.6%降至 2.5%，一度緩解市場對 Fed 緊縮疑慮。惟 7
月 PPI 與 PCE 數據表現，限制利率進一步下行空間。
8 月中旬之後，市場焦點由政策利率逐步轉向能源價格、財政供給與期限溢酬。美伊局勢反覆推

升油價，加上美國聯邦債務8 月首度突破 40 兆美元，2 年與 30 年期利差由 7 月底 99 個基點擴大至
8/18 的 109 個基點，30 年期殖利率亦升至 5.28%。美國財政部 8/19 宣布，自 9/9 起將部分 10～
30 年期公債單次流動性回購上限由 20 億美元提高至至少 40 億美元，帶動 30 年期殖利率由 5.28%
快速回落至 5.19%。截至 8/24，2 年與 30 年利差亦回到 99 個基點，月中陡峭化大致回吐。此一走
勢顯示 ，回購措施雖有助改善長券流動性 ， 但財政赤字 、公債供給及期限溢酬等結構性壓力仍未消失 。
全球主要成熟國家的長端債券亦面臨類似壓力。德國 30 年期公債殖利率近期升至約 3.79%，創
2011 年以來最高；日本 10 年期公債殖利率 8/18 盤中一度觸及 2.945%，亦為 1996 年 9 月以來高
位，30 年期亦一度升至 4.115%。成熟市場長債同步承壓，反映財政擴張、通膨風險與貨幣政策正常
化正共同推升長端期限溢酬。

不過，信用市場整體仍相對穩定，截至 8/24，聖路易聯準銀行的 ICE 美國銀行美國投資級公司
債 OAS 約 81 個基點、有效殖利率約 5.4%，但信用利差仍處歷史偏低區間，緩衝並不寬裕。AI 基礎
建設融資的供給壓力亦已開始反映在定價，Reuters 8/21 指出，2026 年至 8 月上旬主要 AI 雲端業
者發債規模已約 2,200 億美元，科技類投資級債利差升至 89 個基點，較整體 IG 市場高出約 9 個基
點；近期 Alphabet 新券亦需提供約 10～15 個基點讓利，顯示在企業基本面仍穩健之際，大量新券
供給已成為影響信用評價的重要因素。
展望 9 月，美債殖利率預料仍將處於相對高檔，若就業持續降溫且通膨未再明顯升高，中期利率
上行空間可望受限。期限配置方面，短端對 Fed 政策預期較敏感，過短存續期間亦有再投資風險；
10 年以上債券則持續承受財政與期限溢酬壓力。值得注意的是，截至 8/27，10 年與 30 年期殖利率
利差為 52 個基點，2 年至 30 年總利差 96 個基點，顯示 2 年至 30 年期殖利率曲線陡峭幅度中，逾

半來自 10~30 年區間，反映超長端相對 10 年期仍有明顯期限溢酬。相較之下，3～10 年期兼具較
高息收、適度存續期間與利率下行參與空間。整體而言，目前 3～10 年期美國公債及投資級債的風
險報酬條件相對均衡，料具表現機會。

## Document 075: 261005_daiwa_TW-CCL

### Page 1

Summary: After market hours on 5 October 2026, EMC, TUC, and ITEQ released their 
monthly sales results. Our read-through is positive as the three key CCL vendors beat 
sales expectations by 5-15% on better pricing and on-going spec upgrades. Our thesis is 
that the strong AI demand is causing a spill-over impact on the whole CCL industry with 
low-end CCL having c.100% price hike in 2026E and high-end CCL seeing a 40-50% price 
hike due to cost inflation in 2026E. Recent data points showed this thesis remains valid. 
Looking ahead, we expect high-end/low-end CCL to see additional c.10%/20% ASP 
increases in 4Q26E, which could be the key share price catalyst. We believe that recent 
negatives news (including the impact of PTFE and CPO/NPO on the industry) are 
overdone. The impact on high-end CCL from these new technologies is likely to be limited 
from the point of view of Taiwan CCL companies. As such, we remain positive that the 
three names in our CCL coverage will ride on the decent price hike trend given favourable 
supply-demand dynamics.

• EMC (2383 TT, TWD5,700, Buy [1]) 
EMC’s September 2026 revenue was TWD21.5bn (+7% MoM, +169% YoY), which led to 
its 3Q26 sales (TWD60.9bn; +29% QoQ, +142% YoY) reaching 112% of our 3Q26 forecast 
(TWD54.4bn) and 110% of the Bloomberg consensus forecast (TWD55.5bn). We attribute 
the revenue strength to the higher-than-expected price hike and on-going spec upgrades 
(leading to higher ASPs). Management attributes c.50% of the MoM increase in revenue in 
September to the price hike and the other 50% came to product mix optimisation. Looking 
ahead, the company doesn’t rule out the potential to increase its ASP again in 4Q26 (after 
the first time in 2Q26) in order to reflect rising raw material prices since 2Q26. As such, we 
expect revenue to grow at least high teens again on the potential price head as well as 
ongoing spec upgrades.

EMC sees limited impact from PTFE and HC glass-fibre-free CCL. Based on our 
understanding, PTFE is still under certification accounting for only limited layers inside 
specified high-end PCB as of now. As such, its impact on the high-end CCL TAM should be 
limited. As for HC glass-fibre-free CCL, the rigidity of PCB using this type of CCL could be 
a key concern to us.

Lastly, regarding the recent Co-Packaged Optics/Near-Packaged Optics (CPO/NPO) 
news’s negative impact on the CCL TAM, we also believe it to be limited given the likely 
adoption date for CPO of 2030, based on EMC’s forecast. Besides, we believe the PCB 
board inside CPO scale-up switches at that time would still need to support an 800G/1.6T 
transmission speed (or even lower) which would still require high-end PCB transmission 
although the highest transmission bandwidth can increase to 3.2T via optics. Lastly, EMC 
believes the spec of CCL won’t downgrade to M4 in the worst-case scenario but would be 
at least M6. Last but not least, we believe this kind of CPO-connected AI server might only

Given its leading position in the CCL industry and the likely imbalance between supply and

• TUC (6274 TT, TWD1,780, Buy [1]) 
TUC’s September 2026 revenue was TWD6.1bn (+4% MoM, +115% YoY), which led to its 
3Q26 sales (TWD17.9bn; +26% QoQ, +123% YoY) reaching 103% of our 3Q26 forecast 
(TWD17.4bn) and 105% of the Bloomberg consensus forecast (TWD17.1bn). Looking 
ahead, we expect TUC to deliver at least QoQ growth in 4Q26 given 1) a price hike along 
with its peers for high-end CCL (eg. M8) and 2) some shipment delay from 3Q26 given 
flooding in Southeast Asia. For the longer term, the company is promising a continual spec 
upgrade trend in 2027 with new projects using M9-grade CCL (including low dk1, low dk2, 
and Q-glass version) in 2027 for high-end switches, AI servers, and optical transceivers. 
Considering the insufficient capacity in the CCL industry, we expect TUC to further optimise 
its product mix after acquiring more high-end CCL orders in future quarters (vs. a single 
major AI server client in 2026). We have a Buy (1) rating on TUC, with a 12-month TP of 
TWD2,320, based on a target PER of 35x applied to our 1-year forward EPS forecast. Key 
downside risks: 1) weaker-than-expected demand for switches/AI servers, and 2) market 
share loss to CCL peers.

• ITEQ (6213 TT, TWD751, Buy [1]) 
ITEQ’s September 2026 revenue was TWD5.8bn (+15% MoM, +111% YoY), which led to 
its 3Q26 sales (TWD15.6bn; +35% QoQ, +102% YoY) reaching 114% of our 3Q26 forecast 
(TWD13.7bn) and 108% of the Bloomberg consensus forecast (TWD14.5bn). The number 
for 3Q26 was higher than our estimate due likely to a stronger-than-expected price hike 
(c.30% in 3Q26 vs. expectation of 20%). Looking ahead to 4Q26, we expect the company 
to further raise its pricing by 20-30% to factor in cost inflation in raw materials (E-glass) 
and the supply-demand gap. The company also expects the rising supply of e-glass (which 
could lift its utilisation rate from the current level of 75%) to lift its gross margin more 
significantly. For 2027, the company is excited about the new CPU adoption driving rising 
M7 CCL usage and is working on M9 certification with new end customers, which could 
become a key growth driver in 2028. We have a Buy (1) rating on ITEQ. Key downside 
risk: weaker-than-expected regular server demand.

## Document 089: 261006_ml_kingslide

Source PDF: all report pdf/261006_ml_kingslide.pdf

Date: 2026-10-06

### Page 1

King Slide

Maintain Buy: Sep sales -31% MoM from 
high bases; Aug earnings remain solid

Maintain Rating: BUY | PO: 14,888 TWD | Price: 13,100 TWD

Sep sales -31% MoM from high bases; 3Q26 sales in-line 
King Slide’s Sep sales of NT$4.46bn declined 31% MoM (+196% YoY), after the strong 
sales in the past few months (up 17-46% MoM in Apr-Jul and up 1% MoM in Aug). 3Q26 
sales of NT$17.34bn (+60% QoQ, +297% YoY) were 0.5% above BofAe of NT$17.26bn 
and 0.9% below consensus of NT$17.49bn.  
We are not surprised with the MoM sales drop in Sep as (1) the company’s MoM sales 
trend has been volatile (Exhibit 1 and Exhibit 2), (2) sales bases in Apr-Aug were at 
record levels, (3) contribution from Nvidia Rubin GPU projects should be more 
meaningful in 4Q26 after hardware components ramp-up, and (4) partial general server 
orders/demand likely were pushed out due to key component shortage (e.g. memory, 
CPU). 
Solid Aug EPS NT$40.07; pre-tax/net margin 74.6%/59.1% 
In addition to Sep sales, on the same day (6 Oct), King Slide also announced its Aug 
unaudited key financial data as required by local regulations (Exhibit 3). Aug sales were 
NT$6,466mn (+344% YoY), while pre-tax profit was NT$4,826mn (+216% YoY) and net 
income was NT$3,819mn (+212% YoY). EPS came in at NT$40.07 (+212% YoY). Pre-tax 
margin and net margin were 74.6% and 59.1%, respectively.  
Compared to July unaudited figures announced on 14 Aug (see our 17 Aug report), 
including sales of NT$6,407mn, pre-tax margin of 93.4%, net margin of 74.7%, and EPS 
of NT$50.24, we believe the margin and EPS differences mainly reflected FX impact 
under similar sales scale. 
Combining Jul and Aug unaudited results, sales totaled NT$12.87bn and EPS reached 
NT$90.31. Pre-tax margin and net margin were 84.0% and 66.9%, respectively. Jul + Aug 
sales achieved 75%/74% of BofAe/consensus 3Q26 sales. Pre-tax profit, net income, 
and EPS all achieved 79% of BofAe (79.4% pre-tax margin, 62.9% net margin, and 
NT$113.87 EPS) and 77-78% of consensus (80.3% pre-tax margin, 63.1% net margin, 
and NT$117.62 EPS). We also noted that Jul + Aug EPS of NT$90.31 already surpassed 
EPS of NT$74.38 in 2Q26 with similar pre-tax/net margin (84.0%/66.9% in Jul + Aug, vs. 
84.2%/65.4% in 2Q26). 
Maintain Buy; expect a neutral-to-positive market reaction 
We expect a neutral-to-positive market reaction in the near term in view of significant 
MoM sales decline in Sep but also still solid Aug unaudited data. We maintain Buy on 
King Slide as the server rail market leader with broad and diversified client/project base. 
We expect the company to post 3Q26 results after market close on 12 Nov (Thu).

Price objective basis & risk 
King Slide (KGSLF) 
Our PO of NT$14,888 is based on 32x 2027E EPS. We use 32x P/E multiple (2.5SD 
above P/E average since listing) in view of strong AI server market demand, leading 
position in server rail, solid/high margin profiles, and double-digit sales/EPS CAGR in 
2026-28E. Its early step into server market since 2001 with proven track record, much 
larger scale than peer, and 3,600+ patents as strong moats for high/above-peer margins. 
Its above-30x P/E is a similar case to other component sub-sector leaders in Taiwan.

Upside risks to our PO are: (1) even stronger AI server demand, (2) margin upside thanks 
to larger scale and/or better efficiency, (3) better kitchen slides market demand, (4) 
faster capacity expansion, (5) favorable FX movement (mainly USD/NTD) for GM and/or 
non-operating items.

Downside risks to our PO are: (1) softer AI server demand due to GPU/components 
shortage, (2) GM pressure due to higher raw material cost (e.g. steel, aluminum), (3) 
softer kitchen slides market demand under macro uncertainty/weakness, (4) delayed 
product certification, (5) slower capacity expansion, (6) unfavorable FX movement 
(mainly USD/NTD) to weigh on GM and/or non-operating items.

Điểm nhấn thị trường

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX tăng điểm trong tuần qua, 20.45 điểm tương ứng 1.14%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Dầu khí, Bảo hiểm và Tiện ích khí là các nhóm ngành có mức tăng rõ nét nhất trong 
tuần. Khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị 2,680 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, HDB và BSR; đồng thời, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng ở 
mức 1,172 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại mua ròng 3,009 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua và đang 
duy trì vị thế mua lũy kế 5,001 hợp đồng. Tự doanh bán ròng -9,259 hợp đồng và đang duy 
trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng 9,458 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam có tuần tăng điểm tích cực, chỉ số VNINDEX chinh phục thành công 
ngưỡng tâm lý 1.800 điểm với thanh khoản cải thiện, độ rộng thị trường nghiêng về số mã 
tăng điểm và nhà đầu tư nước ngoài quay trở lại mua ròng. Động lực hỗ trợ thị trường 
trong tuần qua đến từ hoạt động cơ cấu của các quỹ bám theo rổ chỉ số mới nổi của FTSE, 
khi mà ngày 21/9 là ngày Việt Nam được chính thức nâng hạng. Thêm vào đó, diễn biến hạ 
nhiệt của giá dầu khi lo ngại thiếu hụt cung do xung đột ở Trung Đông giảm bớt cũng là 
yếu tố hỗ trợ tâm lý thị trường chung. Ở chiều ngược lại, thông tin FED tăng lãi suất có tác 
động tiêu cực nhưng ảnh hưởng không đáng kể lên thị trường do động thái này đã được 
dự báo từ trước.

3

Fed tăng lãi suất, dự báo thêm 
một lần tăng nữa trong năm 
Đánh giá áp lực lên tỷ giá và lãi suất Việt Nam

Fed tăng lãi suất và dự báo có thêm một lần tăng lãi suất nữa trong năm 
— Trong kỳ họp FOMC ngày 15–16/09/2026, Fed quyết định tăng 25bps lãi suất điều hành 
lên mức 3.75%–4%, đánh dấu lần tăng lãi suất đầu tiên kể từ năm 2023. Quyết định 
được thông qua với tỷ lệ đồng thuận 12–0, phản ánh sự thống nhất của các thành viên 
FOMC trong việc ưu tiên kiểm soát lạm phát. 
— Chủ tịch Fed Kevin Warsh nhấn mạnh lạm phát vẫn ở mức cao và cần được đưa về mục 
tiêu 2% trong thời gian phù hợp hơn. Bên cạnh đó, Fed đánh giá hoạt động kinh tế 
đang tăng trưởng vững, tiêu dùng nội địa duy trì tích cực, năng suất và đầu tư vốn tăng 
trưởng mạnh, trong khi thị trường lao động vẫn ổn định. Điều này cho thấy Fed chưa 
nhận thấy áp lực suy giảm kinh tế đủ lớn để ưu tiên nới lỏng chính sách tiền tệ.  Ông 
Warsh không đưa ra cam kết cụ thể về thời điểm tăng lãi suất, nhấn mạnh chính sách 
sẽ phụ thuộc vào diễn biến lạm phát, thị trường lao động và các dữ liệu kinh tế sắp tới. 
— Biểu đồ dot plot tháng 9 cho thấy Fed vẫn nghiêng về khả năng tăng thêm lãi suất 
trong năm 2026, với 16/18 thành viên dự báo ít nhất một lần tăng nữa, đưa lãi suất điều 
hành lên vùng 4%–4.25%. Tuy nhiên, sự phân hóa trong dự báo lãi suất năm 2027 
(3.625%–4.375%) cho thấy đây có thể là một lần điều chỉnh nhằm kiểm soát lạm phát, 
thay vì tín hiệu về một chu kỳ thắt chặt kéo dài.

Quan điểm của KBSV đối với lãi suất và tỷ giá 
— Trong 3 tháng cuối năm, chúng tôi đánh giá nhiều khả năng Fed sẽ có thêm một lần 
tăng lãi suất 25bps, dựa trên:  
(1) Fed tiếp tục nâng dự báo PCE headline và core 2026 lên thêm 10 bps so với dự 
báo hồi tháng 6, đồng thời dự báo lạm phát sẽ quay về mức mục tiêu 2% vào năm 
2029 (thay vì năm 2028 như dự báo trước đó), trong khi dự báo về tỷ lệ thất 
nghiệp và tăng trưởng GDP được điều chỉnh theo hướng tích cực hơn. 
(2) Giá dầu vẫn duy trì ở mức cao (dự kiến quay về vùng 9x USD/thùng vào quý 4) 
trong bối cảnh xung đột tại Trung Đông tiếp tục gây gián đoạn hoạt động vận 
chuyển và xuất khẩu dầu mỏ. Mặc dù Saudi Arabia đã tăng cường vận chuyển 
dầu qua Oman, qua đó giảm bớt lo ngại về thiếu hụt nguồn cung trong ngắn hạn, 
rủi ro gián đoạn vẫn hiện hữu khi xung đột chưa được giải quyết hoàn toàn. 
— Chúng tôi nâng dự báo DXY lên vùng 98–103 điểm trong phần còn lại của năm với động 
lực hỗ trợ USD trong ngắn hạn đến từ việc Fed phát tín hiệu tiếp tục thắt chặt, trong khi 
thị trường điều chỉnh kỳ vọng lãi suất theo hướng duy trì cao hơn. Tuy nhiên, việc các 
NHTW lớn khác cũng duy trì định hướng kiểm soát lạm phát có thể hạn chế mức độ mở 
rộng chênh lệch lãi suất, qua đó làm giảm khả năng USD tăng giá một chiều và DXY 
tăng mạnh khỏi vùng dự báo.

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
96,300 VND/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu Q2/2026 tăng trưởng tích cực 44% YoY, chủ yếu nhờ (1) giá bán khí tăng 
và (2) tăng trưởng sản lượng tích cực từ mảng LNG. Trong khi đó, LNST-CĐTS tăng 
chậm hơn, ở mức 23% YoY, do ghi nhận khoản hoàn nhập dự phòng nợ phải thu khó 
đòi thấp hơn so với cùng kỳ năm trước.

Giá dầu Brent tăng trở lại lên vùng 95 USD/thùng trong bối cảnh xung đột Mỹ - Iran 
tiếp tục leo thang, làm gián đoạn nguồn cung dầu tại Trung Đông, trong khi lưu 
lượng tàu qua Eo biển Hormuz vẫn ở mức thấp và tồn kho dầu toàn cầu cùng Kho 
Dự trữ Chiến lược Mỹ liên tục suy giảm. Chúng tôi kỳ vọng giá dầu duy trì vùng 85 - 
95 USD/thùng trong ngắn hạn, qua đó tiếp tục hỗ trợ biên lợi nhuận mảng khí 
thượng nguồn của GAS nhờ chênh lệch giữa giá bán khí theo giá dầu và giá miệng 
giếng cố định tại các mỏ lâu đời.

Luật Dầu khí sửa đổi tăng ưu đãi cho các mỏ cận biên, giúp cải thiện hiệu quả đầu 
tư và thúc đẩy phát triển nguồn khí mới. GAS có thể hưởng lợi từ sản lượng khí bổ 
sung qua hệ thống hạ tầng hiện hữu, nhưng tác động chủ yếu trong trung và dài 
hạn.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp FCFF và EV/EBITDA để định giá cổ phiếu GAS, chúng tôi đưa 
ra khuyến nghị MUA với mức giá mục tiêu 96,300 VND/cổ phiếu, tương ứng mức 
upside 16.2% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 11/09/2026.

Kết quả kinh doanh tích cực trong 
2Q2026

Giá urea hạ nhiệt, xuất khẩu tiếp tục 
là động lực dẫn dắt doanh thu chính 
của DCM trong 2H2026

Giá khí đầu vào DCM 2028–2030F 
tăng do bổ sung nguồn khí giá cao từ 
Nam Du – U Minh

Luật Dầu khí mới cải thiện triển vọng 
nguồn khí đầu vào cho DCM trong 
dài hạn

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
42,100 VND/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu Q2/2026 tăng 10% YoY, chủ yếu nhờ giá bán urea tăng trong bối cảnh thị 
trường chịu tác động từ các yếu tố địa chính trị. LNST-CĐTS tăng 36% YoY, được hỗ 
trợ bởi giá bán đầu ra tăng nhanh hơn chi phí khí đầu vào.

Giá urea thế giới sau khi tăng cao trong Q2/2026 đã điều chỉnh về vùng trước chiến 
tranh do nhu cầu suy yếu và nguồn cung Trung Quốc quay trở lại. Đối với DCM, xuất 
khẩu urea tiếp tục là động lực dẫn dắt doanh thu chính, trong khi giá và sản lượng 
urea nội địa được kỳ vọng đi ngang trong 2H2026 do đây không phải mùa cao điểm 
tiêu thụ trong nước.

Chúng tôi điều chỉnh tăng 10% dự báo giá khí đầu vào DCM giai đoạn 2028–2030F, 
phản ánh việc bổ sung nguồn khí Nam Du – U Minh từ Q4/2028. Giá khí Nam Du – U 
Minh dự kiến 11.56 USD/mmBTU, cao hơn ~35% so với các nguồn hiện hữu, tạo áp 
lực tăng chi phí đầu vào.

Luật Dầu khí sửa đổi sẽ rút ngắn quy trình phê duyệt FDP và bổ sung ưu đãi cho các 
mỏ khó khai thác, qua đó hỗ trợ đầu tư E&P và duy trì sản lượng dầu khí trong 
nước. Đối với DCM, tác động dự kiến mang tính gián tiếp và có độ trễ, chủ yếu thông 
qua cải thiện nguồn cung khí nội địa, chưa phải động lực thúc đẩy lợi nhuận đáng 
kể trong ngắn hạn.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp FCFF và EV/EBITDA định giá cổ phiếu DCM chúng tôi đưa ra 
khuyến nghị MUA với mức giá mục tiêu 42,100 VND/cổ phiếu, tương ứng mức upside 
29.5% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 11/09/2026.

Doanh thu và LNST-CĐTS 2Q2026 
tiếp tục tăng trưởng tốt, được hỗ trợ 
bởi đà tăng của crack spread

Biên lợi nhuận duy trì ở mức cao 
trong ngắn hạn nhờ crack spread 
bùng nổ, nhưng có thể biến động 
mạnh

Biên lợi nhuận trung và dài hạn chịu 
áp lực từ rủi ro dư cung dầu

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
32,200 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Trong Q2/2026, doanh thu BSR tăng 60% YoY nhờ giá nhiên liệu tăng mạnh trong 
bối cảnh nguồn cung gián đoạn do xung đột Trung Đông. LNST-CĐTS tăng 807% 
YoY, được hỗ trợ bởi crack spread của bốn sản phẩm lọc dầu chủ lực tăng mạnh do 
nguồn cung thắt chặt.

Xung đột quân sự Mỹ – Iran gây gián đoạn nghiêm trọng dòng chảy dầu qua Eo biển 
Hormuz, thắt chặt nguồn cung dầu thô và thành phẩm, đẩy giá Brent lên quanh 100 
USD/thùng và đưa crack spread tại châu Á đạt đỉnh và duy trì ở vùng cao từ tháng 3 
đến nay. Diễn biến crack spread thời gian tới sẽ phụ thuộc đáng kể vào diễn biến 
chiến sự tại Eo biển Hormuz và tốc độ khôi phục lưu thông qua Eo biển này.

Triển vọng lợi nhuận trung và dài hạn của BSR chịu áp lực khi cán cân cung – cầu 
dầu toàn cầu được dự báo chuyển sang dư cung trong trung và dài hạn. Tuy nhiên, 
nhu cầu tái tích lũy tồn kho dầu toàn cầu có thể phần nào hấp thụ lượng cung dư 
thừa và hạn chế mức độ thu hẹp crack spread.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp FCFF và EV/EBITDA định giá cổ phiếu BSR chúng tôi đưa ra 
khuyến nghị Mua với mức giá mục tiêu 32,200 VND/cổ phiếu, tương ứng mức upside 
16.9% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 11/09/2026. Chúng tôi áp dụng mức EV/EBITDA mục 
tiêu là 15.8 lần, bằng với mức chuẩn trung bình 5 năm của cổ phiếu.

BÁO CÁO NGẮN CƠ HỘI ĐẦU TƯ

15/09/2026

CTCP Nhựa Tiền Phong (NTP) 
Tăng tốc trong chu kỳ thuận lợi

Mua 
Giá mục tiêu VND 73.200 
Vùng hỗ trợ/Điểm mua 1 50.000 – 51.400 
Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng 1 44% 
Vùng hỗ trợ/Điểm mua 2 42.000 – 44.000 
Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng 2 70%

Lưu ý: 
Nhà đầu tư cần kết hợp nhận định xu hướng thị 
trường chung, mức độ hấp dẫn của các điểm 
nhấn đầu tư và khẩu vị rủi ro của mình để quản 
lý tỷ trọng tại từng điểm mua cho phù hợp. 
Do việc xác định mỗi điểm mua được dựa trên 
các khung thời gian khác nhau nên chúng tôi 
không đưa ra mức dừng lỗ cố định. Nhìn chung, 
mức dừng lỗ nên được đặt chặt hơn cho điểm 
mua gần (5-7%) và rộng hơn cho điểm mua xa 
(10-15%).

ĐIỂM NHẤN ĐẦU TƯ 
Kỳ vọng sản lượng tiêu thụ tăng trưởng mạnh hơn so với giai đoạn trước. KBSV 
dự báo sản lượng tiêu thụ của NTP tăng 2,3% CAGR giai đoạn 2026 - 2028 nhờ (1) 
Đẩy mạnh giải ngân đầu tư hạ tầng tại miền Bắc (2) gia tăng đầu tư hạ tầng tại các 
địa phương thu hút FDI (3) tăng tốc đơn hàng mảng dự án.

Giá hạt nhựa sẽ gây áp lực biên lợi nhuận nửa cuối 2026 nhưng không làm thay 
đổi triển vọng dài hạn. Mặc dù duy trì chính sách dự phòng nguyên liệu cao và đa 
dạng hóa nhà cung cấp, nhưng NTP vẫn đối mặt với rủi ro thu hẹp lợi nhuận, 
nhưng sẽ không làm giảm đáng kể triển vọng tăng trưởng trung hạn. KBSV cho 
rằng tình trạng dư cung hạt nhựa tại Trung Quốc vẫn sẽ duy trì trong trung hạn và 
xung đột được giải quyết, giảm áp lực biên lợi nhuận toàn ngành.

Catalyst thoái vốn kỳ vọng tái định giá doanh nghiệp. Theo kế hoạch thoái vốn 
Nhà nước, SCIC dự kiến thoái toàn bộ 37,1% cổ phần tại NTP . Chúng tôi đánh giá 
việc hiện thực hóa thương vụ có thể giúp NTP (1) thu hút nhà đầu tư chiến lược 
mới; (2) cộng hưởng mô hình kinh doanh; (3) tái định giá doanh nghiệp.

Hoạt động kinh doanh  Diễn biến doanh thu và lợi nhuận thuần 5 năm gần nhất

NTP là một trong những doanh nghiệp đầu 
ngành ống nhựa tại Việt Nam, với thị phần 
khoảng 31% toàn quốc và 60% tại miền Bắc. 
Hoạt động kinh doanh cốt lõi tập trung vào 
sản xuất và phân phối ống nhựa cùng các sản 
phẩm phụ tùng. Nhờ lợi thế về thương hiệu, 
quy mô và mạng lưới phân phối, NTP duy trì 
vị thế dẫn đầu và nền tảng tăng trưởng ổn 
định qua các năm.

Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KB Securities Vietnam

Tương quan mặt bằng giá  Diễn biến P/E của doanh nghiệp

NTP đang được giao dịch với mức P/E khoảng 
8,3 lần thấp hơn trung bình 5 năm của cổ 
phiếu. Với kỳ vọng doanh nghiệp sẽ bước vào 
pha tăng trưởng mạnh trong 3 năm tới, chúng 
tôi cho rằng P/E fw 2026F ở mức 9,5x lần phù 
hợp với mức dự phóng tăng trưởng 11,6% 
CAGR giai đoạn 2026 – 2028.

Tăng trưởng tín dụng 2026 được dự 
báo khả quan so với cùng kỳ

NIM chịu áp lực trước mục tiêu tăng 
trưởng

Chất lư ợng tài s ản suy gi ảm nh ẹ do 
mặt bằng lãi suất ở mức cao

Khuyến ngh ị: MUA – Giá m ục tiêu 
25,950 VNĐ/CP  
 Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng tăng trưởng tín d ụng 2026 đ ạt 27%, với động lực chính 
đến từ phân khúc KHDN, đ ặc biệt là t ừ nhóm FDI trong b ối cảnh dòng v ốn FDI vào 
Việt Nam và hoạt động xuất nhập khẩu duy trì tích cực. Xét về lĩnh vực, cho vay kinh 
doanh BĐS kỳ vọng sẽ tăng trưởng vượt trội khi ngân hàng tham gia vào thu xếp vốn 
cho các dự án hạ tầng và d ự án BĐS ưu tiên , trong khi các m ảng kinh doanh khác 
tăng trưởng ở mức khả quan.

Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng NIM giảm so với năm 2025 do áp l ực huy động khiến chi 
phí vốn duy trì ở mặt bằng cao, trong khi lãi su ất cho vay dự kiến ở mức cạnh tranh 
để ưu tiên mục tiêu tăng trưởng tín dụng.

Chúng tôi dự phóng tỷ lệ nợ xấu của ngân hàng sẽ tăng 11bps YoY trong bối cảnh lãi 
suất đang neo ở mức cao và kỳ vọng ngân hàng sẽ tiếp tục duy trì chính sách trích 
lập dự phòng thận trọng nhằm củng cố bộ đệm, qua đó hoàn thành mục tiêu duy 
trì tỷ lệ bao phủ nợ xấu ở mức trên 100%.

Dựa trên triển vọng kinh doanh của ngân hàng cũng như đánh giá các y ếu tố rủi ro,

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX cho thấy xu hướng hồi phục trong tuần qua nhưng với diễn biến khá giằng co, 
được thể hiện qua sự hình thành của một mẫu nến "spinning" trên đồ thị tuần. Trên khung 
ngày, bức tranh có phần tích cực hơn khi chỉ số đã cho phản ứng với vùng hỗ trợ quanh 
1790 và hồi phục trở lại, phản ánh sự nhập cuộc khá chủ động của lực cầu. Vùng kháng cự 
gần quanh 1850 bắt đầu khiến áp lực bán có phần gia tăng trong phiên cuối tuần và diễn 
biến rung lắc có thể sẽ còn tiếp diễn. Mặc dù vậy, khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn đang được 
bảo lưu, nhịp giảm điểm vừa qua nhiều khả năng chỉ là bước điều chỉnh tích lũy trước khi 
VNINDEX thử thách lại và có cơ hội vượt vùng đỉnh cũ.

MACRO STRATEGY 
MARKET VIEW 
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK 
MARKETS IN REVIEW 
Portfolio Considerations
We view recent volatility as a rolling
correction rather than the end of
the cycle. We favor diversified Equity

exposure, with potential opportunities
extending beyond Growth to Value,
Small- and Mid-caps, Non-U.S. Equities
(especially Emerging Markets), dividend-
focused and thematic strategies.
We favor adjusting portfolios toward
strategic durations given attractive
elevated nominal and real yields, and see
value in high-quality bonds, particularly
longer-dated municipal bonds.
Consider broader portfolio
diversification with potential
opportunities across industrial metals,

energy, Real/Tangible Assets, Hedge
Funds, private markets and global
strategies to capture themes from AI,
reindustrialization, infrastructure growth
and market dispersion.
All data, projections and opinions are as of the date of this report and subject to change.
I
N THIS  ISSUE
Macro Strategy—The  Yen, 10-Year Yield and Iran: Bessent’s Three-Front War and
the Stakes for
Investors: Scott Bessent may carry the title of Treasury secretary, but
these days he looks more like America’s financial field marshal. His battlefield stretches

from T
okyo, where he is trying to engineer a stronger Japan yen, to the U.S. Treasury
market, where he is battling to keep the 10-year yield from breaking  decisively above
5%, and to T
ehran, where he has become the point man in the escalating U.S. campaign
to squeeze Iran financially and economically.
Ho
w Scott Bessent’s three-front war plays out remains to be seen. A decisive victory on
all three fronts is unlikely since corralling currency and bond markets is next to impossible,

while the whims of geopolitics can lay waste to the best laid plans. That said, however,
the U.S. Secretary of the Treasury has signaled that he is ready to take a very  proactive,
pro-growth approach to his job. That’s the good news. The less encouraging news stems
from the inconsistencies in the overarching objectives of the Treasury secretary.
Market View—Beyond AI’s Resource Footprint, Consider its “Handprint”: Behind
ever
y Artificial Intelligence (AI)-generated response is a vast physical network of data

centers, power plants, transmission lines, cooling systems, semiconductor facilities
and critical-mineral supply chains. As adoption accelerates, growing demand for power,
water, advanced hardware and raw materials could strain grids, raise emissions and
intensify pressure
on local resources. Yet the sustainability conversation should extend
beyon
d AI’s footprint to consider whether its environmental benefits can outweigh its
resource demands.
Thought of the Week—The Beauty of September: Call it an eventful first 13 trading

days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per barrel, diesel prices surpassed
$6
per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield breached 5%, and
the Federal Reserve (Fed) joined the European Central Bank (ECB) and the Bank of Japan
(BoJ) in hiking rates for the
first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has
shed 1.4% since the start of the month. It’s not unexpected that September would be
choppy for U.S. Equities, especially in a midterm election year. The good news is that

this has historically been an attractive entry point. Since 1950, the S&P 500 has never
been
negative in the six or twelve months post-midterm election, boasting average
returns of 13.8% and 15.8%, respectively. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election. A correction amid a strong fundamental economic backdrop
would be a
potential buying opportunity in our view.

### Page 2

2 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The Yen, 10-Year Yield and Iran:  Bessent’s Three-Front War and the Stakes for Investors
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Scott Bessent may carry the title of Treasury Secretary, but these days he looks more like
America’s financial field marshal. His battlefield stretches from Tokyo, where he is trying
to engineer a stronger Japan yen; to the U.S. Treasury market, where he is battling to keep

the 10-year yield from breaking decisively above 5%; and Tehran, where he has become the
point man in the escalating U.S. campaign to squeeze Iran financially and economically.
Make no mistake about it: Secretary Bessent and the Treasury Department are increasingly
an instrument of American economic statecraft, charged not simply with financing the
government and overseeing the financial system, but with managing markets, currencies
and geopolitical adversaries simultaneously. Welcome to the new age of financial warfare.

Front One: Defending the Yen. The first front unfolded over the summer when Secretary
Bessent and the BoJ stealthily intervened in support of the yen, which had fallen to a near
40-year low against the dollar by late July. To arrest the yen’s decline, the U.S. Treasury sold
euros instead of dollars in support of the yen, while pushing the BoJ to make use of the
Fed’s emergency repo facilities. In further support of the yen, the BoJ did what Bessent and

the markets have been pining for, raising its official rate by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%.
Since intervening in the foreign exchange markets, the yen has appreciated by nearly 5%
against the U.S. dollar, alleviating concerns of a disorderly unwind of the yen and giving
Bessent—thus far—an important victory. Defending the yen, of course, isn’t simply about
Japan—it’s about the U.S. and the need for foreign capital inflows.
Japan is one of America’s largest foreign creditors. A persistently weak yen creates

pressure on Tokyo to defend its currency, potentially by selling some of its enormous
holdings of foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys. That prospect didn’t sit well with the
Treasury secretary. At precisely the moment Washington needs foreign capital to finance
its enormous fiscal deficits, the last thing Mr. Bessent wants is one of America’s largest
creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities. Hence, the battle to backstop

Japan’s currency.
Front Two: Defending the 10-Year Bond. Mr. Bessent has had less success on the
second front: the U.S. bond market. While the 10-year Treasury yield slipped slightly
following last week’s decision by the Fed to boost the fed funds rate by 25 bps, the
benchmark interest rate is up roughly 85 bps since the start of the year. Stubbornly high
inflation, elevated oil prices, enormous government borrowing requirements and growing
investor anxiety over America’s fiscal trajectory have all converged and conspired to keep

the 10-year yield closer to 5% than 4%.
Field Marshal Bessent is very aware that the 10-year Treasury is the gravitational center of
global finance—and that mortgage rates, corporate borrowing costs, equity valuations and
the government’s own interest bill all take their cue from it. A sustained move materially
above 5% would tighten financial conditions across the American economy, potentially
threaten the Equity bull market, and pile more pressure on the Republican Party.

To prevent this from happening, the Treasury has expanded its bond-buyback operations,
including purchases of longer-dated government securities, in an effort to improve liquidity
and relieve some pressure on the market, while shifting new government debt issuance
into short-term bills. Yet the bond vigilantes have so far proved harder to intimidate than
currency traders. Even after Treasury announced additional purchases, yields continued
climbing before receiving some relief from the latest hawkish move from the Fed.

Over the medium term, if investors believe Washington is borrowing too much, inflation is
running too hot or the supply of bonds is overwhelming global demand, they will demand a
higher yield to own America’s debt. This battle is far from over.
Front Three: Squeezing Tehran. Then there is Iran, where Mr. Bessent is wielding an
entirely different weapon: America’s control over the plumbing of global finance. In practice
Investment Implications
We expect continued investor

support and preference for real hard
assets, and a bias towards a plethora
of commodities and industrial plays.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
this means the Treasury can make a foreign bank choose between doing business
with Iran or the U.S. It can freeze assets, blacklist companies, restrict transactions and
pressure allies to close financial escape routes.
Under “Operation Economic Outcast,” billed as economic “D-Day,” Treasury has
intensified sanctions against Iranian entities and financial networks, including recent

actions against Iran aviation and additional efforts aimed at banks and organizations
facilitating Tehran’s international transactions. Mr. Bessent has described the objective
in unusually stark terms: to “economically asphyxiate” the Iran regime. This is economic
warfare in its purest form, but whether it works remains to be seen.
“Asphyxiating” an economy takes time and patience—and international cooperation
and coordination. It means closing multiple global loopholes of third-party entities still

willing and able to do business with Iran—look no further than Russian banks. In addition,
squeezing a major oil producer runs the risk of aggravating global oil supplies, keeping
oil prices elevated. Higher energy costs, in turn, feed right back into the U.S. inflation
outlook the Fed is trying to tame and keeps the bond vigilantes on guard and ready to
push back. Not unlike the battle to suppress U.S. bond yields, the battle to bring Iran to
heel is going to be a long one.

How does it all end? How Scott Bessent’s three-front war plays out remains to be seen. A
decisive victory on all three fronts is unlikely since corralling currency and bond markets
is next to impossible, while the whims of geopolitics can lay waste to the best-laid plans.
That said, however, the U.S. Treasury Secretary has signaled that he is ready to take
a very proactive, pro-growth approach to his job. That’s the good news. The less
encouraging news stems from the inconsistencies in the overarching objectives of the

Treasury secretary.
Mr. Bessent can pressure Iran because access to the dollar-based financial system
remains extraordinarily valuable. He can intervene in foreign-exchange markets because
America’s financial resources give Washington enormous clout. And he can attempt to
influence the Treasury market because U.S. government securities remain the foundation
of the global financial system. But therein lies the contradiction.
The U.S. is simultaneously weaponizing the financial system abroad while becoming more

dependent on that same financial system at home. Washington wants the world to fear
losing access to dollars while also asking the world to lend it trillions of dollars. It wants to
use Treasury and dollar dominance as instruments of geopolitical coercion while running
fiscal deficits that require foreigners to continue believing deeply in those very assets.
The more Washington borrows, the more sensitive it becomes to bond-market discipline.
The more aggressively it weaponizes access to the dollar, the greater the incentive for

adversaries to seek alternatives. And the more the Treasury secretary intervenes directly
in markets, the more investors may begin wondering where economic policy ends and
market management begins.
Exhibit 1: Scott Bessent's Three-Front War.
The Front The Problem Bessent's Objective The Weapon
The Yen Weak yen/currency instability Stronger yen Foreign Exchange intervention, policy coordination, pressure on Japan
The Yield Rising 10-year Treasury yield Lower/contained yields Debt-management strategy, buybacks, issuance policy

Source: Chief Investment Office.
For the moment, Mr. Bessent is fighting on three fronts. He wants a stronger yen, a
lower 10-year Treasury yield and an economically isolated Iran. Success on any one front
would be difficult. Achieving all three simultaneously will require considerable skill—and
perhaps a little luck.

MARKET VIEW
Beyond AI’s Resource Footprint, Consider its “Handprint”
Anna Potts, CFA, Vice President and Investment Strategist
Since the launch of ChatGPT, investors have celebrated AI’s potential while growing
more concerned about its resource footprint: Behind every AI-generated response is
a physical network of data centers, power plants, transmission lines, cooling systems,

semiconductor facilities and critical-mineral supply chains. No doubt, the AI revolution
has environmental costs. As adoption accelerates, growing demand for power, water,
advanced hardware and raw materials could strain grids, raise emissions and intensify
pressure on local resources. Yet the sustainability conversation should extend beyond
AI’s footprint to consider whether its environmental benefits can outweigh its
resource demands.
AI’s footprint is large, growing and certainly not trivial. AI increases electricity demand,

adds large, concentrated loads to already constrained grids, and may slow the retirement
of polluting energy assets. U.S. data centers could account for 20% of national electricity
consumption by 2035, up from 5.9% today, with power needs reaching 194 gigawatts
(comparable to the electricity demand of more than 150 million homes). 1 Cooling, power
generation and semiconductor manufacturing require water; each new megawatt also
embeds substantial copper, aluminum and other materials; and short hardware cycles

can increase electronic waste. Use varies sharply by workload: Complex reasoning and
agentic tasks can consume many times more energy than a simple text prompt. The
effects are already reaching consumers and communities. Wholesale power costs on
PJM—the largest U.S. grid—rose 76% year-over-year in Q1 2026, while data-center
load included in its previous two capacity auctions added an estimated $13.8 billion to
customer bills. 2 Communities are mobilizing around their concerns, including affordability,

water use and air pollution: Q1 2026 produced the largest single quarterly concentration
of delayed or blocked data-center projects on record. 3 Opposition is therefore becoming
a development constraint to be considered.
Context, caveats and comparisons. Headlines often tell only part of the story. Globally,
data centers account for about 1% of electricity use, 4 modest relative to many industries,
and 9% of estimated global 2025 to 2030 electricity growth. 5 A typical text prompt on

one major company’s AI model 6 uses 0.24 watt-hours of electricity, emits 0.03 grams
of CO₂ and consumes 0.26 milliliters of water—roughly 617,000 prompts match the
emissions from producing and cooking one sirloin steak, while 262,000 match the water
used in an eight-minute shower. 7 Still, billions of interactions, more complex workloads
and growth in resource-constrained regions can create meaningful cumulative impacts.
The most meaningful assessment therefore depends on workload, utilization, location,

grid mix, cooling design, local water availability and who bears the cost of supporting
infrastructure, not one universal number.
AI’s Handprint. While much remains to be seen, AI’s environmental “handprint”—the
benefits it can enable—could potentially prove larger than its direct footprint in some
areas. Across the energy system, AI can help utilities better match electricity supply
and demand, balance grids, optimize storage and reduce wasted energy. Data centers

already account for more than 30% of corporate renewable power-purchase agreements,
helping create long-dated demand for clean generation. 8 Beyond power, AI can improve
precision agriculture, streamline transportation and inventories, and support product

Responsible AI growth requires
investment in power generation,

grid modernization, energy storage,
advanced cooling, water treatment
and reuse, efficient computing, and
materials recovery and recycling.
Diversified environmental and thematic
strategies may offer exposure to
these physical enablers while reducing
reliance on benchmark names.

design, predictive maintenance, waste sorting and recycling. AI is transforming climate
risk management by using satellite imagery, weather data and predictive analytics to
forecast floods, wildfires and extreme weather events, enabling governments, companies,
and investors to anticipate risks and allocate capital more effectively (Exhibit 2). These
gains are not automatic: they depend on innovation, adoption, and whether efficiency

improvements reduce total resource use rather than simply stimulate more demand. All
industries are faced with the opportunity to responsibly use AI to make operations more
efficient, and much is yet to be discovered.

AI’s potential handprint becomes more tangible in transportation, agriculture and climate
resilience. In aviation, for example, AI can combine weather forecasts, flight paths and
atmospheric conditions to reroute aircraft around regions where warming contrails
are most likely to form—reducing climate impact without requiring a new aircraft or

fuel system. On farms, machine learning can interpret aerial imagery and field-level
sensor data to identify crop stress early, directing water, fertilizer and chemicals to
where they are needed and helping growers improve yields while reducing inputs. AI
can also strengthen climate preparedness by analyzing satellite imagery, weather data
and infrastructure exposure to detect deforestation, anticipate floods or wildfires,
and identify communities, facilities and supply chains most vulnerable to physical

hazards. These are just a few examples that illustrate how AI’s value can extend beyond
incremental efficiency gains to reshape real-world decisions.
Investment Considerations. The challenges created by AI also create potential
investment opportunities. Companies are investing in efficient chips, closed-loop cooling,
reclaimed-water systems, energy storage, grid upgrades and cleaner firm power to
reduce resource intensity and improve reliability. Professionally managed environmental

and thematic strategies can target these “picks and shovels” of the digital buildout,
alongside businesses using AI to improve efficiency across energy management,
water infrastructure, environmental services, waste management and compute. A
disciplined thematic approach can diversify exposure beyond the largest benchmark
constituents while positioning for structural growth in power demand, grid modernization
and resource efficiency. Focusing solely on AI’s footprint risks overlooking both the

companies helping it scale more efficiently and the broader environmental benefits its
handprint may enable.

THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
The Beauty of September
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Call it an eventful first 13 trading days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per
barrel, diesel prices surpassed $6 per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S.
Treasury yield breached 5%, 9 and the Fed joined the ECB and the BoJ in hiking rates for

the first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has shed 1.4% since the start
of the month, while its equal-weighted counterpart is down by 3.3%.
It’s not all too unexpected that September would be a choppy month for U.S. Equities.
Historical seasonal patterns suggest as much. Since 1928, the average S&P 500 return
in September has come out to -1.1%, 10 not only the worst month of the year on average
but also the only month when the benchmark has ended lower more often than it’s

ended higher. Similar weakness has been seen in midterm election years, with the S&P
500 producing an average September return of -0.8% since 1950. 10
The good news is that this has historically been an attractive entry point for U.S. Equity
investors. Call it the beauty of September: In contrast to September’s average decline,
average returns improve in October (+3.0%), November (+2.7%), and December (+0.8%).
Indeed, the S&P 500 has never been negative in the six or 12 months post-midterm

election since 1950 (Exhibit 4A), boasting an impressive average return of 13.8% and
15.8% over those respective periods. 10
Looking ahead to the coming weeks, we have little reason to believe that 2026
will diverge from the historical trend. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election, in line with what’s typical in the months surrounding the
midterms (Exhibit 4B). A quieter corporate calendar ahead of Q3 earnings means

investor focus will remain on incoming macro data, the path of energy prices, and the
Fed’s policy path.
In the meantime, while the S&P 500 still stands just 2.7% below its mid-August high,
churn beneath the surface already appears to be taking place. Just 38% of S&P 500
members now stand above their 50-day moving average versus 70% a mere month ago. 9
Far from worrisome, a correction in the near term in the context of a strong fundamental
economic backdrop would be a potential buying opportunity in our view.

## Document 206: Consumer Checkpoint_ Still sizzling August spending remained robust as consumers sought value, income groups converged and household finances stayed healthy. 2 min read

Economy 
Consumer Checkpoint: Still sizzling 
14 September 2026

Key takeaways 
• Consumer spending remained solid in August. Total card spending per household rose 0.9% month-over-month and 4.5% year-
over-year, according to Bank of America internal data. Discretionary services continue to provide a steady foundation for this 
growth, despite moderation in a few categories. 
• Consumers still appear to be focused on value. Spending at general merchandise and big-box retailers continues to outperform 
more traditional retailers, as evidenced during the back-to-school season. This suggests households are actively seeking lower-
cost alternatives while maintaining spending. 
• Spending and wage growth have largely converged across income groups, though across generations, the largest "K" shape 
remains among Millennials. Household finances also remain healthy, with credit card utilization declining across most age and 
income cohorts. Lower- and middle-income households still hold elevated deposit balances across all age groups, suggesting 
spending should remain resilient.

A summer of spending  
Consumer spending looked very robust in August. Bank of America total aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household jumped 0.9% month-over-month (MoM) on a seasonally-adjusted basis, following the 0.2% dip in July. And card 
spending rose 4.5% year-over-year (YoY), more than four times the average growth rate of 2025. Excluding gasoline, spending 
was up 3.7% YoY, almost 2.5 times the rate in 2025. Additionally, while August YoY growth rates have eased over the past two 
months, they remained above the first three months of 2026 (Exhibit 1).

Spending growth outpaced transaction growth across most discretionary categories 
Within discretionary categories there is some variation, though YoY spending growth has improved since the beginning of the 
year across most (Exhibit 3). Leisure and travel-related spending (lodging and airlines) have seen the largest improvements, likely 
in part reflecting the impact of the World Cup over the summer.

But rising global oil prices have also pushed up costs in areas like travel. Looking at transactions as a guide to spending volumes,

Consumers continue to pivot to big-box stores and wholesalers 
One “stand-out” among discretionary spending categories is general merchandise, where robust and rising transaction growth 
has largely mirrored its spending growth, up 4.7% and 5.6% YoY, respectively.   
In our view, this partly reflects consumers searching for value to offset the increase in gas prices, among other items (read more 
in The rise of value). This trend extends to back-to-school spending. In fact, while spending at traditional back-to-school retailers 
(like teen and children’s apparel) has declined this year, outlays have ramped up at general merchandise stores (Exhibit 6). This 
suggests to us that back-to-school spending was likely solid this year but just shifted to different channels.  
Exhibit 6: Solid spending growth for general merchandise, while more traditional back-to-school retailers, like teen and children’s apparel, have 
experienced a slight decline

Spending and wages keep converging, but it differs slightly by age 
Looking across income cohorts, total card spending growth has largely converged in recent months, effectively closing the “K” 
shape in overall spending (Exhibit 7). This is also true in discretionary spending: Bank of America card data shows lower-income 
households’ discretionary spending growth is recovering strongly. In August, discretionary spending rose 5.9% YoY for higher-
income households versus 5.7% YoY for lower-income counterparts, the smallest gap since January 2024 (Exhibit 8). Meanwhile, 
middle-income households’ discretionary spending also improved but lagged other income cohorts slightly, albeit still up a 
healthy 5.1% YoY.  
The closing gap in spending growth between higher- and lower-income households has been mirrored in strengthening after-tax 
wage growth (read more in The Institute Employment Report: August 2026). Lower-income households' wage growth outpaced 
that of other cohorts in August for the second consecutive month (Exhibit 9). 
Exhibit 7: Lower-income households’ total card spending growth is 
stronger than all income cohorts

I NSTI TUTE

While the “K-shaped” divergence in discretionary credit and debit card spending has closed, it likely persists in higher-cost items 
and services such as vehicles and moving (read more in Autos: In gear, but the “K” is still in the headlights and On the move: 
Renovation over relocation?).  
We see two reasons for this. First, these types of purchases may require saving for down payments or larger borrowing (and may 
be more interest rate-sensitive), so it’s possible recent wage growth has yet to translate into bigger-ticket buys. Second, some 
of the improvement in after-tax wage growth may be due to temporary factors like lower tax withholdings, meaning some 
consumers may not feel comfortable signing up for higher-dollar and longer-term financial commitments.  
Gen Z, Millennials and Gen X have less room to borrow, but elevated deposits 
When it comes to overall financial health, we believe consumers remain on solid footing. One sign of this is that more consumers 
are paying off their entire credit card balance every month, both compared to last year and to pre-pandemic levels (read more in 
Consumer Checkpoint: The great convergence). And for those who do carry a balance (revolvers) from month to month, overall 
utilization rates have declined YoY and are near pre-pandemic levels (Exhibit 11).  
Exhibit 11: Credit card utilization for revolvers is on par with pre-pandemic levels and has declined YoY 
Median credit card utilization for credit card revolvers (monthly, index 2019 average = 100, below 100 means there has been a decrease relative to 2019 levels)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Looking at utilization rates for revolvers by age and income, Baby Boomers have more room to borrow than younger generations 
across all income cohorts. Even in August 2026, Gen Z, Millennial and Gen X utilization rates are still well above Baby Boomer’s 
2019 utilization rates (Exhibit 12). The good news is that most of these younger generations have seen borrowing capacity 
increase slightly since then. Millennials, however, at the margin have made the least progress, with utilization rates still at 2019 
levels.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts.  
Durables spending is defined as spending on electronics, building materials, auto-related spending and furniture. Premium durables spending is 
based on a selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively higher value products. Conversely, value durables spending is based on a 
selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively lower value products.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

Earnings above all 
• Higher oil prices are in a tussle with stronger growth. We expect strong growth 
to help the global economy weather inflation risks, allowing equities to focus on the 
strength of earnings growth. Oil prices remain the key risk to monitor 
• Maintain a preference for equities over bonds and cash.  Strong earnings 
growth means we would use the recent pause to add exposure to our preferred 
markets and themes. The US and Asia ex-Japan remain our preferred regions 
• Take advantage of higher bond yields via credit risk but cap duration. The 
risk of higher bond yields looks most pressing in ultra -long maturities, but credit 
quality remains strong. We are Overweight EM USD bonds 
Hits from the 1990s 
Rising bond yields have been a key market theme.  Since mid -year, 10 -year 
government bond yields have risen approximately 40-80bps across the US, Japan and 
Euro area. Against this backdrop, gold has delivered high single -digit returns, while 
global equities have delivered low single-digit returns. 
Higher oil prices are in a tussle with robust growth data . The lack of progress 
towards a negotiated settlement in the US -Iran conflict and rising fears of shipping 
restrictions extending to the Bab al-Mandab Strait have meant that oil prices are once 
again testing the USD 100/bbl level. These worries have been exacerbated by 
increasing worries about refined product (eg. diesel, jet fuel, etc) supply. Despite these 
concerns, US growth data has held up remarkably well, as illustrated by the latest PMI 
and retail sales data. This is underpinned by AI investment growth.  
Real, rather than nominal, energy prices are key.  A chart of inflation -adjusted 
energy prices, though, illustrates why the global economy has managed to hold up 
despite these risks. Oil prices in real (inflation-adjusted) terms are closer to long term 
averages rather than at true ‘oil shock’ levels. This, together with the global economy’s 
lower oil intensity relative to history, is why we expect  growth and inflation to avoid 
worst-case scenarios as long as nominal oil prices avoid an excursion into the USD 
120-150/bbl range.

The Fed is likely to hike twice more by mid -2027. The 
acceleration in AI-driven investment growth means we believe 
the chances of a shift from our core soft-landing to no-landing 
economic scenario have gone up. This, combined with still -
high inflation readings mean we now expect the Fed to hike 
rates twice more by mid -2027. In contrast, the BoJ and ECB 
are expected to hike thrice and once more respectively. 
Buy-the-dip in equities 
1990s rate hikes a better parallel then 2022 . There is 
understandably some concern about whether rising central 
bank policy rates, and rising bond yields across maturities, will 
present a challenge to equity markets. However, we believe 
rate hikes in the 1990s are a better historical parallel, when 
central banks were hiking rates into strong economic and 
earnings growth, resulting in equity markets maintaining their 
pace of gains. The 2022 hiking cycle, in contrast, represented 
an inflation surprise, which we believe is not the case today. 
Strong earnings growth overwhelms other factors today. 
Still-strong economic and earnings growth means that we 
remain constructive on the outlook for equities and other risky 
assets going into year -end and beyond. The pause over the 
last couple of months was likely driven by a combination or 
rising bond yields , September seasonality and excessive 
investor optimism around mid-year.  
However, earnings expectations for global equities remain 
very strong (especially in our preferred US and Asia ex-Japan 
regional markets). We believe these earnings remain 
sufficiently robust to outweigh the drag from higher bond 
yields. Therefore, w e remain Overweight equities relative to 
bonds and cash, though we believe it is prudent to ensure the 
size of the equities Overweight is not excessive. Within Asia, 
we also trim China equities to a core holding, leaving Taiwan 
as our only regional Overwei ght, given the ongoing focus on 
AI investment-driven markets for now. 
Bumps remain part of the journey. As we laid out in our mid-
year outlook, the second half of this year is expected to be 
characterised by positive, but bumpy returns as a result of the 
factors laid out in the previous section, but also US mid -term 
election risks. The second half has thus far played out as 
expected given sufficient bumps, but still robust earnings 
growth. We would not be surprised by some volatility around 
US mid-term elections, but any further pullbacks or pauses 
would be opportunit ies to raise exposure given strong 
earnings growth is expected to ultimately dominate. 
Prefer credit over duration 
Corporate credit quality remains strong . Aggregate 
indicators of credit quality suggest credit quality remains 
strong across both Investment Grade and High Yield bonds 
across most regions. US IG bond yields face some upward 
pressure from significant hyperscaler supply, but supply -
driven spread widening tends to be relatively short -lived in 
nature and should not signal significant credit quality worries. 
Longer-maturity bonds remain at risk of further volatility. 
In contrast, we continue to be concerned that the volatility in 
very long maturity bonds could extend. Continued inflation 
and debt worries mean it is possible investors continue to 
demand a greater ‘term premium’ (ie. a premium for holding 
bonds with very  long maturities). We continue to believe 
limiting bond duration to 3-7 years remains appropriate.  
We continue to prefer  corporate and EM over government 
bonds. This implies earning a higher yield by taking on more 
credit risk (via corporate or EM bonds) rather than adding 
more duration risk. Within bonds, we remain Overweight EM 
USD bonds and view IG and HY bonds as core holdings. 
Modest USD weakness; gold to grind higher 
USD bounce led by pricing of Fed hikes . However, we 
believe Fed hikes are now excessively priced. At the same 
time, we see room for non-US rates to rise further, especially 
in Japan where the undervalued JPY is likely to strengthen. 
This is why modest USD weakness is likely to resume. 
Gold to continue grinding higher. While the move higher is 
likely to be characteri sed by two -steps-forward-one-step-
back, we believe an ultimately softer USD and continued EM 
central bank demand should result in continued gains in the 
precious metal over both the next 3- and 12-month periods.

2 Foundation: Our tactical asset allocation

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Summary

With the Federal Reserve’s latest decision to raise its benchmark rate by 25 basis points in September 2026, as well as the one 
to two additional hikes predicted  before the end of the year, we are entering a new interest -rate hiking cycle. This marks an 
important shift for investors, but higher policy rates do not necessarily imply weaker portfolio returns. What matters is why rates 
are rising, how quickly they increase, and whether economic growth can absorb tighter financial conditions. 
Our framework places the current environment in a healthy/high-yield regime. Growth remains resilient, credit spreads are 
tight and the yield curve is normalising. At the same time, the economy is approaching an important boundary. Inflation remains 
above target and the 10-year US government bond yield has reached 5%. A renewed rise in inflation or a sharper slowdown in 
activity could move markets into an unhealthy/high-yield regime. 
This argues for  measured risk-taking rather than an aggressively defensive stance . We maintain a slight overweight in 
equities, hold a strong overweight in gold and a neutral allocation to fixed income. Diversification remains key near a regim e 
boundary, and hedge funds can provide useful sources of return that are less dependent on the direction of traditional markets.

Not all interest-rate hiking cycles are the same

Traditional market 
analysis often treats 
rising rates as uniformly 
negative. History 
suggests a more 
nuanced conclusion.  
We classify hiking 
cycles along two 
dimensions: whether  
the increase in rates is 
healthy or unhealthy, 
and whether it occurs  
in a high- or low-yield 
environment. 
 Fig. 8 The different regimes of interest-rate hiking cycle 
Historical interest-rate hiking cycle defined by real growth and US 10-Year treasury yields

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; Since 1974

A healthy increase in rates is generally associated with firm 
demand, above -trend growth, resilient equities and stable 
credit markets. Central banks can move gradually and remain 
data-dependent because the economy is strong enough to 
withstand tighter fin ancial conditions. An unhealthy increase 
is more likely to be driven by supply constraints or persistent 
inflationary pressures. Growth slows, credit spreads widen, 
equities de-rate and central banks are forced to react more 
aggressively. 
The second dimension is the starting level of yields. In a high-
yield environment, bonds offer meaningful income and a 
greater cushion against moderate price declines. In a low -
yield environment, that protection is limited. This distinction 
matters because the same increase in yields can have very 
different portfolio consequences depending on the income 
available at the outset.
